  "old_string": "\\textbf{Remark.}\\;%\nPart~\\textit{(i)} relies on source attribution (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), the mechanism used by SICA.\nUnder \\emph{semantic} evaluation (checking whether $c_i$ is logically consistent with answer $a$ directly), $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ additionally requires \\emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \\text{ supports } a_i) \\geq P(c_i \\text{ supports } a')$ for $a' \\neq a_i$.\nThis property is structurally motivated (the reasoning chain producing $a_i$ generates intermediate steps aligned with $a_i$) and is empirically validated across all nine model--task conditions ($\\mathrm{BR} \\in [1.66,\\, 4.97]$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), but is not a mathematical necessity of self-extraction alone; it could fail if $f$ systematically extracted premises that contradict their source trace's conclusion.\nUnder the degeneration condition (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), which holds on 94.4\\% of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), these conclusions extend to the complete SICA scoring function.\n\nUnder SICA's source attribution (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), Proposition~1\\textit{(i)} implies $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ deterministically for every problem; the empirical BR (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}), which evaluates semantic support across incorrect problems, consistently exceeds this structural bound ($1.66\\times$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).",
  "new_string": "\\textbf{Remark.}\\;%\nProposition~1 relies on source attribution (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), the scoring mechanism used by SICA.\nUnder \\emph{semantic} evaluation (checking whether $c_i$ is logically consistent with answer $a$ directly), $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ additionally requires \\emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \\text{ supports } a_i) \\geq P(c_i \\text{ supports } a')$ for $a' \\neq a_i$.\nThis property is structurally motivated (the reasoning chain producing $a_i$ generates intermediate steps aligned with $a_i$) and is empirically validated across all nine model--task conditions ($\\mathrm{BR} \\in [1.66,\\, 4.97]$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), but is not a mathematical necessity of self-extraction alone; it could fail if $f$ systematically extracted premises that contradict their source trace's conclusion.\nUnder the degeneration condition (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), which holds on 94.4\\% of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), these conclusions extend to the complete SICA scoring function.\n\nProposition~1 implies $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ deterministically for every problem under source attribution; the empirical BR (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}), which evaluates semantic support across incorrect problems, consistently exceeds this structural floor ($1.66\\times$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), reflecting both the base-rate effect of trace-count distributions and extraction alignment."
}
```

  "new_string": "Decision boundaries ($C_0 < 5\\%$, $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$, SC $< 40\\%$) were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B conditions. }

{

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Trained verifiers \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd} sidestep this with external reward signals. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot}, trained verification signals \\citep{chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but to our knowledge, none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot}, trained verification signals \\citep{chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but to our knowledge, none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. \\citet{kamoi2024selfcorrect} survey when LLMs can self-correct, finding that external feedback is typically necessary. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Training-based verification.}\nThe methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers introduce external reward signals that can escape the self-extraction bottleneck: outcome reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}, process reward models \\citep{lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, and dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025} all introduce supervision beyond the model's own traces. In the code domain, AlphaCode \\citep{li2022alphacode} and CodeT \\citep{chen2023codet} use execution-based filtering as a form of independent verification. Proposition~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers introduce external reward signals that operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Corollary~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption."
}

_source line 14242 · 2026-05-23T08:48:55.441855+00:00_

797	\section{TOST Equivalence Tests}
798	\label{app:tost}
799	
800	We apply two one-sided tests (TOST) \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with equivalence margin $\pm 2$\,pp to all 16 non-degenerate SICA--SC comparisons, classifying each condition as \emph{equivalent} (TOST significant), \emph{genuinely different} (McNemar $p < 0.05$ and TOST not significant), or \emph{underpowered} (neither test significant). Four conditions are equivalent, confirming that SICA and SC produce statistically indistinguishable accuracy. Two conditions show genuine differences, both negative: Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). The remaining ten conditions are underpowered, reflecting the limited statistical power at $n{=}200$--$204$ for detecting $|\Delta| \leq 2$\,pp effects.
801	
802	\paragraph{Margin sensitivity.} Table~\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity} shows that the classification is robust across margins: regardless of the chosen margin, no condition shows a significant positive SICA effect.
803	
804	\begin{table}[h]
805	\centering
806	\small
807	\caption{TOST classification sensitivity across equivalence margins (16 non-degenerate conditions).}
808	\label{tab:tost-sensitivity}
809	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
810	\toprule
811	Margin & Equivalent & Different & Underpowered \\
812	\midrule
813	$\pm 1$\,pp & 0 & 2 & 14 \\
814	$\pm 2$\,pp & 4 & 2 & 10 \\
815	$\pm 3$\,pp & 10 & 0 & 6 \\
816	\bottomrule
817	\end{tabular}
818	\end{table}
819	
820	\paragraph{Relative-margin TOST.}
821	\label{app:relative_tost}
822	The absolute $\pm 2$\,pp margin is more stringent at high baselines ($\pm 2.4\%$ relative at SC $= 85\%$) than low ones ($\pm 5.1\%$ at SC $= 39\%$). A relative $\pm 5\%$ of SC margin addresses this asymmetry: 8 conditions are equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), and 6 underpowered. The additional four equivalent conditions are all high-SC ($\geq 75\%$), where the wider relative band captures practical ind

_source line 14264 · 2026-05-23T08:50:23.242563+00:00_

150	\label{sec:diagnostics}
151	
152	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation method from a $K = 12$ pilot run: (1) the degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2) the diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity for any aggregation gain); (3) the bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$ with BR $> 1$ predicts confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ predicts error amplification). These boundaries correctly classify all 60 tested conditions (16 main configurations, 27 remediation variants on Mistral-7B${\times}$FOLIO, and 17 analytical conditions) within the self-extraction paradigm.
153	
154	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries ($C_0 < 5\%$, $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$, SC $< 40\%$) were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B conditions. We evaluate prospectively on two held-out generators: LLaMA-3.1-8B (different family, not used in boundary derivation) on FOLIO (SC $= 66.18\%$, $C_0 = 61.8\%$, $K_\text{eff} = 2.17$), where the framework predicts Type~A confirmation bias ($\Delta \leq 0$) and the observed $\Delta = -4.41$\,pp confirms; and Qwen3-14B (same family as Qwen2.5, different generation) on FOLIO and ProofWriter, where predictions are likewise confirmed ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp, n.s.).

_source line 14266 · 2026-05-23T08:50:26.657078+00:00_

11	\looseness=-1
12	Symbolic confirmation bias applies beyond SICA to any method extracting structured representations from LLM traces. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior when the verification signal originates from the same model. Formal soundness is necessary but not sufficient; extraction must be independent. Our findings provide independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations identified by \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}, and extend the analysis to formal verification: even provably correct formal solvers become vacuous when their inputs inherit the model's bias.
13

1	\looseness=-1 Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters with self-extraction; frontier models confirm SCB universality but use cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B, as closed-source APIs do not expose internals---whether self-extraction on frontier models would yield different BR values remains untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks; we do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, multilingual settings, or commonsense domains lacking gold annotations. NLI verification was tested only on propositional/first-order logic tasks; domain-adapted NLI amplifies gains dramatically but ProofWriter's synthetic templates contribute to the high verifier accuracy; generalization to naturalistic benchmarks and non-logical reasoning domains remains untested. Syntactic deduplication (46.0\% removal rate) may retain semantically equivalent constraints; however, the shuffle control (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) preserves the same duplication pattern and yields $\text{BR}_\text{shuffle} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$, suggesting deduplication granularity does not drive the observed bias direction. McNemar power is below 21\% for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed; multi-seed replication on Mistral-7B (3 seeds) and Qwen2.5-14B (4 seeds) FOLIO confirms the single-seed patterns ($\Delta$ range spans ${\sim}6$\,pp and ${\sim}2$\,pp, respectively; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}), with multi-seed data for remaining conditions in progress. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions sharing verifier components ($p = 0.167$ after collapsing); we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. SICA requires $4.5\times$ the wall-clock time of SC with no significant accuracy gain. Additional scope considerations are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, particularly in mathematical reasoning \citep{byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$. Symbolic confirmation bias is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether reasoning reflects actual computation, while symbolic confirmation bias concerns whether self-extracted formal constraints mirror the model's answer distribution.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extract
…[truncated 3448 chars]

  "old_string": "The methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers introduce external reward signals that can escape the self-extraction bottleneck: outcome reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}, process reward models \\citep{lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, and dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025} all introduce supervision beyond the model's own traces. In the code domain, AlphaCode \\citep{li2022alphacode} and CodeT \\citep{chen2023codet} use execution-based filtering as a form of independent verification. Proposition~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers introduce external reward signals that operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Corollary~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.",
  "new_string": "The methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \\citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Proposition~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Corollary~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption."

44	\medskip\noindent
45	\textbf{Proposition 1} \textit{(Source-Attribution Degeneration).}\;%
46	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ be reasoning traces from model $M$ on question $q$.
47	Assume answers are trace-determined: $a_i = h(t_i)$.
48	Let $\hat{a} = \mathrm{mode}(a_1, \ldots, a_K)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
49	Under the source-attribution scoring of Eq.~\ref{eq:score}, the constraint score reduces to the majority-vote count: $\operatorname{score}(a) = n_a$.
50	When $|\{a : n_a > 0\}| \geq 2$, the bias ratio satisfies $\mathrm{BR} \triangleq n_{\hat{a}} \big/ \max_{a' \neq \hat{a}} n_{a'} \geq 1$.
51	
52	\medskip
53	\textit{Proof.}\;%
54	By definition, $\operatorname{score}(a) = \sum_{i=1}^K \mathbf{1}[a_i = a] = n_a$, maximized at $\hat{a}$, so $\mathrm{BR} \geq 1$.\hfill$\square$
55	
56	\medskip\noindent
57	\textbf{Corollary 1} \textit{(Information Ceiling).}\;%
58	If additionally constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), let $Y$ denote the ground truth. Then
59	$I(C_{1:K};\, Y) \leq I(T_{1:K};\, Y)$ and $I(C_{1:K};\, Y \mid A_{1:K}) \leq I(T_{1:K};\, Y \mid A_{1:K})$:
60	self-extracted constraints cannot carry more information about $Y$ than the source traces, even beyond what the answers already encode. This bound holds for \emph{any} model $M$, task $q$, and deterministic extraction function $f$, and applies to any self-extraction pipeline, not only SICA.
61	
62	\medskip
63	\textit{Proof.}\;%
64	$c_i = f(t_i)$ makes $C_{1:K}$ a deterministic function of $T_{1:K}$, so $Y \mathop{\text{---}} T_{1:K} \mathop{\text{---}} C_{1:K}$ forms a Markov chain and the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K}; Y) \leq I(T_{1:K}; Y)$.
65	The conditional bound follows identically: $c_i = f(t_i)$ holds unconditionally, so the Markov property and DPI apply within each stratum $\{A_{1:K} = \mathbf{a}\}$.\hfill$\square$
66

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly classifying all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to a held-out model family (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling practitioners to evaluate viability from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before committing to the $4.5\times$ computational overhead of full deployment.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}. With $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$ across instruction-tuned models, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information. Effective scaling requires either structurally independent verifiers (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) or trained signals that escape the model's distributional prior \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm}. The capability-gap pattern further implies that as generators improve, the bar for useful verification rises correspondingly.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Symbolic confirmation bias applies beyond SICA to any method that self-extracts structured representations from the generating model's own traces. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior when the verification signal originates from the same model. Formal soundness is necessary but not sufficient; extraction must be independent. Our findings provide independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations identified by \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}, showing that even provably

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
  "old_string": "Escaping the bottleneck requires three conditions: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}, where the verifier shares neither architecture nor training data with the generator; (ii)~\\emph{capability ordering}, where the verifier exceeds the generator's accuracy on the target domain; and (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}, as evidenced by the $14\\times$ amplification from domain-adapted fine-tuning (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Whether trained extractors or multi-modal verification can shift the independence-accuracy boundary remains open. More broadly, this work reveals a structural limitation of self-referential verification that has been largely overlooked: the bottleneck lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway. The diagnostics and theoretical framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront, rather than rediscovering it through expensive negative results.",
  "new_string": "Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (the verifier shares neither architecture nor training data with the generator), capability ordering (the verifier exceeds the generator on the target domain), and task-verifier alignment ($14\\times$ amplification from domain-adapted fine-tuning; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Whether trained extractors or multi-modal verification can shift the independence-accuracy boundary remains open. More broadly, this work reveals a structural limitation of self-referential verification that has been largely overlooked: the bottleneck lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway. The diagnostics and theoretical framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront, rather than rediscovering it through expensive negative results."
}
```

  "content": "\\looseness=-1 Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters with self-extraction; frontier models confirm SCB universality but use cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B, as closed-source APIs do not expose internals. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks; we do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, multilingual settings, or commonsense domains lacking gold annotations. McNemar power is below 21\\% for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed, with multi-seed replication on Mistral-7B (3 seeds) and Qwen2.5-14B (4 seeds) FOLIO confirming the single-seed patterns. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions; we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. Additional scope considerations are discussed in Appendix~\\ref{app:limitations-extended}.\n"
}
```

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly classifying all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to a held-out model family (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling practitioners to evaluate viability from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before committing to the $4.5\times$ computational overhead of full deployment.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}. With $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information; effective scaling requires structurally independent verifiers (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) or trained signals \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm}, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle extends beyond SICA: self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior when verification originates from the generating model. Our findings provide independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}, showing that even provably correct solvers become vacuous when their inputs inherit the model's bias.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (the verifier shares neither architecture nor training data with the generator), capability ordering (the verifier exceeds the generator on the target domain), and task-verifier alignment ($14\times$ amplificatio

1	\looseness=-1 Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters with self-extraction; frontier models confirm SCB universality but use cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B, as closed-source APIs do not expose internals. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks; we do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, multilingual settings, or commonsense domains lacking gold annotations. McNemar power is below 21\% for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed, with multi-seed replication on Mistral-7B (3 seeds) and Qwen2.5-14B (4 seeds) FOLIO confirming the single-seed patterns. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions; we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. Additional scope considerations are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, particularly in mathematical reasoning \citep{byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$. Symbolic confirmation bias is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether reasoning reflects actual computation, while symbolic confirmation bias concerns whether self-extracted formal constraints mirror the model's answer distribution.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extract
…[truncated 3066 chars]

1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Corollary~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.) and $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction, $m{=}12$); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp, $p > 0.4$). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. Preliminary evidence suggests that zero-shot NLI escape requires the generator's SC to fall below the verifier's standalone accuracy.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifi
…[truncated 2471 chars]

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\nEscaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (the verifier shares neither architecture nor training data with the generator), capability ordering (the verifier exceeds the generator on the target domain), and task-verifier alignment ($14\\times$ amplification from domain-adapted fine-tuning; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Whether trained extractors or multi-modal verification can shift the independence-accuracy boundary remains open. More broadly, this work reveals a structural limitation of self-referential verification that has been largely overlooked: the bottleneck lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway. The diagnostics and theoretical framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront, rather than rediscovering it through expensive negative results.",

More broadly, this work reveals a largely overlooked structural limitation: the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway. The diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this upfront, rather than rediscovering it through expensive negative results."

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\nThis independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. With $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information; effective scaling requires structurally independent verifiers (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) or trained signals \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm}, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}: even provably correct solvers become vacuous when their inputs inherit the model's bias.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nEscaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment ($14\\times$ amplification from domain-adapted fine-tuning; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors or multi-modal verification can shift this boundary remains open. More broadly, this work reveals a largely overlooked structural limitation: the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway. The diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this upfront, rather than rediscovering it through expensive negative results.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\nThis independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. More broadly, the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway---the diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/solutions.tex",
  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three generators above the verifier (SC $\\geq 72\\%$) show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp). These results point to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern}: verification gain is governed by a capability ordering between verifier and generator, decreasing monotonically with generator accuracy and reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), suggesting it reflects a structural property of independent verification rather than an artifact of a particular architecture. Per-label analysis confirms that gain derives from error complementarity that diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp). These results point to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern}: verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), suggesting a structural property of independent verification rather than an architecture artifact. Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli})."
}
```

McNemar power is below 21\\% for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed, with multi-seed replication (3 seeds on Mistral-7B, 4 on Qwen2.5-14B) confirming single-seed patterns. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions; we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. _source line 14380 · 2026-05-23T09:04:21.851307+00:00_

60	self-extracted constraints cannot carry more information about $Y$ than the source traces, even beyond what the answers already encode. This bound holds for \emph{any} model $M$, task $q$, and deterministic extraction function $f$, and applies to any self-extraction pipeline, not only SICA. The contribution is not the inequality itself but the identification of the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain structure in self-extraction pipelines, establishing that constraints cannot carry information about $Y$ beyond what $A$ already provides.
61	
62	\smallskip\noindent
63	\textit{Proof.}\;%
64	$c_i = f(t_i)$ makes $C_{1:K}$ a deterministic function of $T_{1:K}$, so $Y \mathop{\text{---}} T_{1:K} \mathop{\text{---}} C_{1:K}$ forms a Markov chain and the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K}; Y) \leq I(T_{1:K}; Y)$.
65	The conditional bound follows identically: $c_i = f(t_i)$ holds unconditionally, so the Markov property and DPI apply within each stratum $\{A_{1:K} = \mathbf{a}\}$.\hfill$\square$
66	
67	\smallskip\noindent
68	\textbf{Remark.}\;%
69	Proposition~1 relies on source attribution (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), the scoring mechanism used by SICA.
70	Under \emph{semantic} evaluation (checking whether $c_i$ is logically consistent with answer $a$ directly), $\mathrm{BR} \geq 1$ additionally requires \emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \text{ supports } a_i) \geq P(c_i \text{ supports } a')$ for $a' \neq a_i$.
71	This property is structurally motivated (the reasoning chain producing $a_i$ generates intermediate steps aligned with $a_i$) and is empirically validated across all nine model--task conditions ($\mathrm{BR} \in [1.66,\, 4.97]$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), but is not a mathematical necessity of self-extraction alone; it could fail if $f$ systematically extracted premises that contradict their source trace's conclusion.

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1 Four representative failure modes illustrate this trade-off. \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp) successfully breaks trace correlation but collapses accuracy: same-model diversity and quality are at odds. \\emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM as extractor) decreases $\\kappa$ slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because same-family models share systematic biases (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). \\emph{Contrastive prompting} produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con constraints), indicating confirmation bias transfers to constraint generation. \\emph{Iterative constraint generation}, which refines constraints across multiple extraction rounds, reduces surface-level duplication but the refined constraints still align with the model's majority answer.\n\n\\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling observed across three families. This ceiling is not a property of any single extraction method but of the model itself: regardless of prompting strategy, self-extraction cannot produce constraints that are independent of the model's distributional prior. The oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp with gold constraints) proves the mechanism is sound; self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\%, localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality rather than the MAX-SAT apparatus.\n\n\\looseness=-1 No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). The consistent failure across 27 variants, four categories, and three model families suggests that symbolic confirmation bias is not a fixable extraction artifact but an intrinsic property of self-referential constraint generation within the extraction-aggregation paradigm.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 Four representative failure modes illustrate this trade-off. \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy. \\emph{Cross-model extraction} decreases $\\kappa$ slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because same-family models share systematic biases (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). \\emph{Contrastive prompting} produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con), indicating confirmation bias transfers to constraint generation. \\emph{Iterative refinement} reduces surface duplication but refined constraints still align with the majority answer.\n\n\\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families: regardless of prompting strategy, self-extraction cannot produce constraints independent of the model's distributional prior. The oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp with gold constraints) proves the mechanism is sound; self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\%. No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}), suggesting symbolic confirmation bias is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation within the extraction-aggregation paradigm."
}

### User

_source line 14450 · 2026-05-23T09:47:45.569649+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] Round 17 审稿修复。EMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL 2026-05-25。14 项修改，按优先级排列。编译后正文必须 ≤8 页。引用不需要验证。

## 优先级 1（Critical — 修复后可从 3.0 升至 3.5）

### Fix 1: Proposition 1 证明重写 [W1, 复发×2]
**文件**: analysis.tex L44-74
**问题**: 当前证明声称 score(a) = Σ 1[aᵢ=a] = nₐ，但 Eq.3 的 score 对约束 c 求和（带权重 wc），不是对 trace i 求和。这是数学错误。
**修改**:
1. Proposition 1 改为 **argmax preservation**：
   "Under source-attribution scoring (Eq.3) and the degeneration condition (Eq.degenerate, |C⁻|≈0), the SICA answer selection reduces to majority vote: argmax_a score(a) = argmax_a n_a."
2. 证明中显式列出假设链：(a) degeneration: |C⁻|≈0; (b) source attribution: σ(c,a)=1 iff c 来自答案为 a 的 trace; (c) 约束权重在同答案组内等比例分布 → argmax 不变
3. 补充经验验证一句：如 "Table 10 confirms: MAX-SAT and count-only aggregation agree on 94.4% of problems."
4. Proposition 1 后的 "Corollary 1" → 改为 "Remark 1 (Information Ceiling)"（见 Fix 5）

### Fix 2: 标题 + Abstract + Table 1 Power 标注 [W2, 复发×7]
**文件**: main.tex L40, abstract.tex, experiments.tex (Table 1)
**问题**: 10/16 条件 underpowered 但标题/Abstract 用全称否定。第7次被 flag，之前纯改措辞不够。
**修改**:
1. 标题改为：`Beyond Self-Consistency: Self-Extracted Constraints\\Do Not Improve LLM Logical Reasoning`（去掉 "Substantially"，更简洁直接）
2. Abstract 中 "We show the answer is no" → "We find no evidence of improvement"

_source line 14454 · 2026-05-23T09:48:05.020994+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We show the answer is no. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on 94.4\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp, though ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp (absence of evidence is not evidence of absence). We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer, with bias ratios from $1.66\times$ to $4.97\times$ across nine models (7B to frontier scale), reflecting both the base-rate effect of trace-count distributions and extraction alignment. We prove the voting degeneration is structural under source-attribution scoring (Proposition~1) and confirm it via shuffle control, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations identified by \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary evidence suggests that structurally independent cross-architecture verification can escape the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics correctly classify all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, enabling practitioners to predict a priori whether symbolic aggregation will help.
2

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp.
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Proposition~1) and show it

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
5	\label{sec:pipeline}
6	\label{sec:condorcet}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), effective sample size reduces to
10	\begin{equation}
11	\label{eq:effk}
12	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
13	\end{equation}
14	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}), motivating constraint-based aggregation.
15	
16	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
19	
20	\looseness=-1
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves thi
…[truncated 3589 chars]

1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Corollary~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
13

15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). 1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly classifying all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to a held-out model family (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling practitioners to evaluate viability from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. More broadly, the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway---the diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront.
10

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, particularly in mathematical reasoning \citep{byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$. Symbolic confirmation bias is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether reasoning reflects actual computation, while symbolic confirmation bias concerns whether self-extracted formal constraints mirror the model's answer distribution.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extract
…[truncated 3066 chars]

  "old_string": "\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Proposition 1} \\textit{(Source-Attribution Degeneration).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ be reasoning traces from model $M$ on question $q$.\nAssume answers are trace-determined: $a_i = h(t_i)$.\nLet $\\hat{a} = \\mathrm{mode}(a_1, \\ldots, a_K)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder the source-attribution scoring of Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}, the constraint score reduces to the majority-vote count: $\\operatorname{score}(a) = n_a$.\nWhen $|\\{a : n_a > 0\\}| \\geq 2$, the bias ratio satisfies $\\mathrm{BR} \\triangleq n_{\\hat{a}} \\big/ \\max_{a' \\neq \\hat{a}} n_{a'} \\geq 1$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Proof.}\\;%\nBy definition, $\\operatorname{score}(a) = \\sum_{i=1}^K \\mathbf{1}[a_i = a] = n_a$, maximized at $\\hat{a}$, so $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$.\\hfill$\\square$\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Corollary 1} \\textit{(Information Ceiling).}\\;%\nIf additionally constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), let $Y$ denote the ground truth. Then\n$I(C_{1:K};\\, Y) \\leq I(T_{1:K};\\, Y)$ and $I(C_{1:K};\\, Y \\mid A_{1:K}) \\leq I(T_{1:K};\\, Y \\mid A_{1:K})$:\nself-extracted constraints cannot carry more information about $Y$ than the source traces, even beyond what the answers already encode. This bound holds for \\emph{any} model $M$, task $q$, and deterministic extraction function $f$, and applies to any self-extraction pipeline, not only SICA. The contribution is not the inequality itself but the identification of the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain structure in self-extraction pipelines, establishing that constraints cannot carry information about $Y$ beyond what $A$ already provides.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Proof.}\\;%\n$c_i = f(t_i)$ makes $C_{1:K}$ a deterministic function of $T_{1:K}$, so $Y \\mathop{\\text{---}} T_{1:K} \\mathop{\\text{---}} C_{1:K}$ forms a Markov chain and the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K}; Y) \\leq I(T_{1:K}; Y)$.\nThe conditional bound follows identically: $c_i = f(t_i)$ holds unconditionally, so the Markov property and DPI apply within each stratum $\\{A_{1:K} = \\mathbf{a}\\}$.\\hfill$\\square$",
  "new_string": "\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Proposition 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace within each answer group, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Proof.}\\;%\nBy~(i), $\\mathcal{C}^* \\approx \\mathcal{C}$ and the penalty term vanishes: $\\operatorname{score}(a) = \\sum_{c:\\, \\sigma(c,a)=1} w_c$. By~(ii), each constraint contributes only to its source answer. By~(iii), total weight for answer $a$ is proportional to $n_a$, preserving the argmax. Table~\\ref{tab:ablation} confirms: MAX-SAT and count-only aggregation agree on 94.4\\% of problems.\\hfill$\\square$\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Remark 1} \\textit{(Information Ceiling).}\\;%\nIf constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), then $Y \\mathop{\\text{---}} T_{1:K} \\mathop{\\text{---}} C_{1:K}$ forms a Markov chain, and the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K};\\, Y) \\leq I(T_{1:K};\\, Y)$: self-extracted constraints cannot carry more information about $Y$ than the source traces. This bound holds for any model, task, and deterministic $f$, applying to any self-extraction pipeline. The contribution is not the inequality itself---a direct DPI application---but the identification of the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain structure, establishing that constraints carry no information about $Y$ beyond what $A$ already provides."
}

  "replace_all": "False",

  "old_string": "\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Remark.}\\;%\nProposition~1 relies on source attribution (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), the scoring mechanism used by SICA.\nUnder \\emph{semantic} evaluation (checking whether $c_i$ is logically consistent with answer $a$ directly), $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ additionally requires \\emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \\text{ supports } a_i) \\geq P(c_i \\text{ supports } a')$ for $a' \\neq a_i$.\nThis property is structurally motivated (the reasoning chain producing $a_i$ generates intermediate steps aligned with $a_i$) and is empirically validated across all nine model--task conditions ($\\mathrm{BR} \\in [1.66,\\, 4.97]$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), but is not a mathematical necessity of self-extraction alone; it could fail if $f$ systematically extracted premises that contradict their source trace's conclusion.\nUnder the degeneration condition (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), which holds on 94.4\\% of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), these conclusions extend to the complete SICA scoring function.\n\nProposition~1 implies $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ deterministically for every problem under source attribution; the empirical BR (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}), which evaluates semantic support across incorrect problems, consistently exceeds this structural floor ($1.66\\times$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), reflecting both the base-rate effect of trace-count distributions and extraction alignment.",
  "new_string": "\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Remark 2.}\\;%\nProposition~1 assumes degeneration and source attribution. Under \\emph{semantic} evaluation, the equivalence additionally requires \\emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \\text{ supports } a_i) \\geq P(c_i \\text{ supports } a')$ for $a' \\neq a_i$, which is structurally motivated and empirically validated ($\\mathrm{BR} \\in [1.66,\\, 4.97]$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), but could fail if $f$ systematically extracted premises contradicting their source trace's conclusion.\nSince Proposition~1 shows SICA reduces to majority vote under degeneration, $\\mathrm{BR} \\geq 1$ follows from the trace-count geometry; the empirical BR consistently exceeds this floor ($1.66\\times$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), reflecting both base-rate effects and extraction alignment."
}

_source line 14485 · 2026-05-23T09:53:56.697537+00:00_

called Write

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — Write</summary>

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
  "content": "Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on 94.4\\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Proposition~1)---and the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.\n"
}
```

</details>

  "old_string": "Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly classifying all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to a held-out model family (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling practitioners to evaluate viability from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment.",
  "new_string": "Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly characterizing failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment. Our experimental validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA extraction-aggregation paradigm; the theoretical bounds (Proposition~1, Remark~1) apply to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline by construction, but empirical confirmation beyond SICA remains future work."
}
```

    "old_string": "Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly classifying all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to a held-out model family (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling practitioners to evaluate viability from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment.",
    "new_string": "Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly characterizing failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment. Our experimental validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA extraction-aggregation paradigm; the theoretical bounds (Proposition~1, Remark~1) apply to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline by construction, but empirical confirmation beyond SICA remains future work."
  }
}

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}\nSelf-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \\citep{loo2025reevaluating}, particularly in mathematical reasoning \\citep{byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}). \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \\citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\\kappa$. Symbolic confirmation bias is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether reasoning reflects actual computation, while symbolic confirmation bias concerns whether self-extracted formal constraints mirror the model's answer distribution.",

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
  "old_string": "Proposition~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints are d-separated from ground truth given the answer ($C \\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct in natural language without external feedback; our findings provide independent evidence consistent with this limitation in the domain of formal verification, showing that provably sound solvers add no information beyond majority voting when constraints are self-extracted.",
  "new_string": "Proposition~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our findings provide independent evidence consistent with this limitation in formal verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. The latter operates at the \\emph{trace level}: individual reasoning chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation. SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted formal constraints mirrors the answer distribution rather than providing independent verification signal. Methodologically, CoT bias is established via observational behavioral analysis; SCB is formalized through a Markov chain structure (Proposition~1, Remark~1) and confirmed via systematic exclusion of 27 training-free alternatives (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The key difference in conclusion: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful, while SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints---formally correct by construction---carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted."
}
```

</details>

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly characterizing failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment. Our experimental validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA extraction-aggregation paradigm; the theoretical bounds (Proposition~1, Remark~1) apply to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline by construction, but empirical confirmation beyond SICA remains future work.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. More broadly, the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway---the diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront.
10

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly characterizing failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment. Our experimental validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA extraction-aggregation paradigm; the theoretical bounds (Proposition~1, Remark~1) apply to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline by construction, but empirical confirmation beyond SICA remains future work.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. More broadly, the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway---the diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront.
10

1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). 1	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. The $\alpha$-insensitivity confirms degeneration: under $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ the penalty term vanishes and $\alpha$ becomes irrelevant, producing $\geq$99\% agreement across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ as a ceiling effect (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), confirming that observed BR is fully explained by the trace-count base rate. The bias is structural precisely because it is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Proposition~1), not an artifact of extraction.
10	
11	\looseness=-1 The pattern generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.85\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaM

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. The three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle, extending beyond SICA to any self-extraction-based aggregation method and correctly characterizing failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm. Decision boundaries generalize prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}), enabling failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run ($K{=}12$) before full deployment. Our experimental validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA extraction-aggregation paradigm; the theoretical bounds (Proposition~1, Remark~1) apply to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline by construction, but empirical confirmation beyond SICA remains future work.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, and the capability-gap pattern implies the bar rises as generators improve. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. More broadly, the bottleneck of self-referential verification lies not in the formal reasoning channel but in the extraction pathway---the diagnostics and framework we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront.",
