The theoretical bounds (Proposition~1, Remark~1) apply to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline by construction; empirical validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, providing independent evidence consistent with \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. The diagnostics we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront."

```json
  "old_string": "Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. The latter operates at the \\emph{trace level}: individual reasoning chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation. SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted formal constraints mirrors the answer distribution rather than providing independent verification signal. Methodologically, CoT bias is established via observational behavioral analysis; SCB is formalized through a Markov chain structure (Proposition~1, Remark~1) and confirmed via systematic exclusion of 27 training-free alternatives (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The key difference in conclusion: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful, while SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints---formally correct by construction---carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted.",
  "new_string": "Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. Methodologically, SCB is formalized via a Markov chain structure (Proposition~1, Remark~1) and confirmed through systematic exclusion of 27 training-free alternatives (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The key distinction: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful; SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted."
```
```json
}
```

### User

_source line 14566 · 2026-05-23T10:03:33.511590+00:00_

[Director 指令] [补充数据] Agent 计算完成，以下数据用于 Fix 1 和 Fix 6。

## Fix 1 补充：Argmax Agreement Rate

SICA score argmax 与 trace-count argmax 的 agreement rate：

| Condition | Agreement | N |
|-----------|-----------|---|
| Mistral-7B × FOLIO | 92.2% | 204 |
| Mistral-7B × PW | 90.2% | 600 |
| Qwen2.5-14B × FOLIO | 97.5% | 204 |
| Qwen2.5-14B × PW | 93.2% | 600 |
| LLaMA-8B × FOLIO | 87.3% | 204 |
| LLaMA-8B × PW | 87.3% | 600 |
| Overall | 90.8% | 2412 |

在 Proposition 1 证明中使用：
- "Empirically, SICA's answer selection agrees with majority vote on 90.8% of problems across six conditions (range: 87.3%–97.5%), confirming that source-attribution scoring approximately preserves majority-vote ranking under the degeneration condition."
- 注意：90.8% 不等于 94.4%（degeneration condition 覆盖率）。94.4% 是约束完全退化的比例，90.8% 是最终 argmax 一致的比例——差异来自 tiebreaking。

## Fix 6 补充：BR Base-Rate Decomposition

| Condition | Observed BR | Base-rate BR | Excess |
|-----------|------------|-------------|--------|
| Mistral × FOLIO | 2.19 | 3.85 | −1.65 |
| Mistral × PW | 2.46 | 4.44 | −1.98 |
| Qwen × FOLIO | 2.05 | 4.37 | −2.31 |
| Qwen × PW | 1.53 | 3.79 | −2.25 |
| LLaMA × FOLIO | 2.14 | 3.71 | −1.57 |
| LLaMA × PW | 1.49 | 2.83 | −1.35 |

**关键：Excess 全部为负**。约束评分的 BR 低于纯 trace-count 的 BR。

Fix 6 的 framing 调整：
- 不要说 "no excess bias"（这暗示 excess ≈ 0），而是说 "SICA scoring compresses but does not amplify the trace-count base rate: observed BR is consistently lower than base-rate BR across all six conditions (mean excess: −1.85), indicating that deduplication and MaxSAT processing attenuate rather than exacerbate the majority-answer advantage."
- 这**强化**了论文论点：formal verification 不仅没有添加独立信息，它甚至略微压缩了 trace-count bias，但仍不够突破 BR > 1 的结构性下界。
- Shuffle control (p > 0.7) 仍然适用：BR 的方向完全由 trace distribution 决定。

## 额外发现：Disagreement Asymmetry
SICA 与 SC 不一致时，SC 正确率远高于 SICA（139 vs 53）。可在 §5.1 或 Appendix 中提及作为约束引入噪声的补充证据。

_source line 14568 · 2026-05-23T10:05:09.650759+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on 94.4\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Proposition~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2

1	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. The $\alpha$-insensitivity confirms degeneration: under $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ the penalty term vanishes and $\alpha$ becomes irrelevant, producing $\geq$99\% agreement across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ as a ceiling effect (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), confirming that observed BR is fully explained by the trace-count base rate. The bias is structural precisely because it is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Proposition~1), not an artifact of extraction.
10	
11	\looseness=-1 The pattern generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.85\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaM

[Director 指令] [数据修正] Reviewer 发现两个不一致，请按以下修正执行 Fix 1 和 Fix 6。

## Fix 1 修正：Argmax Agreement 数据使用方式

**问题**：论文 analysis.tex 的 94.4% 来自 Table 10 ablation（MAX-SAT vs count-only, 可能是 Mistral-7B FOLIO 单条件），Agent 计算的 90.8% 是 6 条件 overall argmax agreement。两者定义略有不同。

**修正**：
- Prop 1 证明中**不要混用 94.4% 和 90.8%**
- 94.4% 继续在 ablation 上下文中使用（Table 10 引用）
- Prop 1 argmax preservation 的经验验证用 Agent 的数据："Empirically, SICA's answer selection agrees with majority vote on 87%–98% of problems across six model-dataset conditions (90.8% overall; Table X in Appendix)"
- 或更简洁："SICA and majority vote select the same answer on over 90% of problems across all tested conditions"

## Fix 6 修正：跳过精确数值分解，用 shuffle control

**问题**：Agent 计算的 "Observed BR" 是 SICA score ratio，论文 BR 定义是语义支持概率比（Eq.5）。两者不是同一个量（4.97 vs 2.19）。重新计算需要时间，DDL 紧迫。

**修正**：
- **不要使用 Agent 的 BR decomposition 数字**（observed BR 2.19, base-rate BR 3.85 等）
- **直接用已有的 shuffle control 作为 permutation null evidence**："A permutation null model—randomizing constraint-answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1,000 permutations)—yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that observed BR is fully explained by the trace-count base rate."
- 这不需要新数据，只需要更显式的 framing（从"validates that BR reflects trace-count distribution"改为"permutation null model confirms..."）
- 可选：脚注补充 "Under permutation, excess bias $\text{BR}_\text{obs} - \text{BR}_\text{null}$ is not significantly different from zero, indicating that extraction does not amplify the majority-answer advantage beyond what trace counts predict."

## 额外可用数据：Disagreement Asymmetry

Reviewer 确认这是高质量数据。如有空间（一句话即可）：
- "When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer 2.6× more often (139 vs 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal."
- 放在 §5.1 或 §4 Results 中。

7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. The $\alpha$-insensitivity confirms degeneration: under $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ the penalty term vanishes and $\alpha$ becomes irrelevant, producing $\geq$99\% agreement across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ as a ceiling effect (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that observed BR is fully explained by the trace-count base rate; deduplication and MAX-SAT processing do not amplify the majority-answer advantage. The bias is structural: it is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Proposition~1), not an artifact of extraction.
10	
11	\looseness=-1 The pattern generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.85\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\% bootstrap CIs

51	By~(i), $\mathcal{C}^* \approx \mathcal{C}$ and the penalty term vanishes: $\operatorname{score}(a) = \sum_{c:\, \sigma(c,a)=1} w_c$. By~(ii), each constraint contributes only to its source answer. By~(iii), total weight for answer $a$ is proportional to $n_a$, preserving the argmax. Empirically, SICA's answer selection agrees with majority vote on 90.8\% of problems across six conditions (range 87.3--97.5\%; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}).\hfill$\square$

_source line 14598 · 2026-05-23T10:09:07.444360+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on 90.8\% of problems (range 87.3--97.5\% across six conditions) spanning six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Proposition~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), providing independent evidence consistent with the self-correction limitations of \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2

5	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. No condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.112$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided).

10	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). No condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\%$ for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}.}

_source line 14626 · 2026-05-23T10:40:09.056920+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] R18 审稿修复（Round 18 Overall 3.5 → 目标 4.0）。EMNLP 2026 DDL 5月25日。这次不用验证引用。

以下是 17 项具体修改指令，按优先级排列。**每项修改必须精确到文件和位置。**

---

## Critical (3 项)

### Fix 1: 标题软化 + 去掉 "Logical"（W1+W8 合并）
**文件**: `main.tex` line 39
**当前**: `Beyond Self-Consistency: Self-Extracted Constraints\\Do Not Improve LLM Logical Reasoning`
**改为**: `Beyond Self-Consistency: Self-Extracted Constraints\\Show No Evidence of Improving LLM Reasoning`
**理由**: (a) "Do Not Improve" 是全称否定 vs 10/16 underpowered; (b) "Logical" 与实际覆盖（含常识/数学）不匹配

### Fix 2: Proposition 1 降格为 Observation 1（W2）
**文件**: `analysis.tex` lines 44-51
**当前**: `\textbf{Proposition 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}` + proof using ≈
**改为**: `\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}`，把 "Proof" 改为 "Empirical justification"。
原因：假设 (iii) "approximately uniform constraint yield" 无数学定义，证明全程用 ≈ 而非 =。降格为经验性观察更诚实。
**注意**：全文所有引用 "Proposition~1" 的地方都要改为 "Observation~1"。涉及文件：`abstract.tex`, `introduction.tex`, `analysis.tex`, `related_work.tex`, `discussion.tex`, `solutions.tex`, `method.tex`（如果有的话），以及所有 appendix。用全文搜索确保无遗漏。

### Fix 3: SCB 相对于 Huang 的定位从 "consistent with" 改回 "extends"（W3）
**文件**: `introduction.tex` line 9
**当前**: "we provide independent evidence consistent with \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}"
**改为**: "we provide independent evidence that extends \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification"
**文件**: `discussion.tex` line 9
**当前**: "providing independent evidence consistent with \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}"
**改为**: "extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings: even provably sound formal verification inherits the model's distributional prior when constraints are self-extracted"
**文件**: `related_work.tex` line 10 末尾
**当前**: "our findings provide independent evidence consistent with this limitation in formal verification"
**改为**: "our findings extend this limitation to formal self-verification, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting"
**理由**: R18 指出 "consistent with" 是自我降级。§2 已有三维度对比段落支撑 "extends" 定位。

---

## Major (4 项)

### Fix 4: Remark 1 + C⊥Y|A 澄清（W4 + R18-N1）
**文件**: `analysis.tex` lines 54-55 (Remark 1)
当前 Remark 1 只说 DPI bound I(C;Y) ≤ I(T;Y)。审稿人说这对任何确定性后处理都成立，接近 trivial。
**修改方案**: 保留 Remark 1 但重写：
(a) 承认 DPI bound 本身是标准的
(b) 贡献在于识别了 M→T→A→C 马尔可夫链结构
(c) 在 Observation 1 条件下（degeneration），约束在给定答案后不携带真值的额外信息：I(C;Y) ≤ I(A;Y)
(d) 明确说 Figure 3 的 C⊥Y|A 是在 degeneration 条件下成立的经验性结论，不是无条件的

### Fix 5: BR 重新框定为 degeneration 度量（W5 + R18-N2）
**文件**: `analysis.tex`
BR 应从 "bias 度量" 重新框定为 "structural coupling / degeneration indicator"。
具体修改：

1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp.
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Proposition~1) and show it

44	\smallskip\noindent
45	\textbf{Proposition 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
46	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
47	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace within each answer group, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
48	
49	\smallskip\noindent
50	\textit{Proof.}\;%
51	By~(i), $\mathcal{C}^* \approx \mathcal{C}$ and the penalty term vanishes: $\operatorname{score}(a) = \sum_{c:\, \sigma(c,a)=1} w_c$. By~(ii), each constraint contributes only to its source answer. By~(iii), total weight for answer $a$ is proportional to $n_a$, preserving the argmax. SICA and majority vote select the same answer on over 90\% of problems across all tested conditions.\hfill$\square$
52	
53	\smallskip\noindent
54	\textbf{Remark 1} \textit{(Information Ceiling).}\;%
55	If constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), then $Y \mathop{\text{---}} T_{1:K} \mathop{\text{---}} C_{1:K}$ forms a Markov chain, and the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K};\, Y) \leq I(T_{1:K};\, Y)$: self-extracted constraints cannot carry more information about $Y$ than the source traces. This bound holds for any model, task, and deterministic $f$, applying to any self-extraction pipeline. The contribution is the identification of the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain structure, establishing that constraints carry no information about $Y$ beyond what $A$ already provides.
56	
57	\smallskip\noindent
58	\textbf{Remark 2.}\;%
59	Proposition~1 assumes degeneration and source attribution. Under \emph{semantic} evaluation, the equivalence additionally requires \emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \t

_source line 14644 · 2026-05-23T10:41:03.730714+00:00_

46	\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \emph{bias ratio}:
47	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mirage}
48	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
49	\end{equation}
50	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies the degree to which extracted constraints align with the majority answer; values above 1 are expected under source-attribution scoring (Proposition~1) and reflect both the base-rate effect of trace-count distributions and any additional extraction alignment. $\text{BR} = 1$ indicates unbiased extraction. We validate this metric empirically in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.
51

```json
  "old_string": "\\textbf{Remark 1} \\textit{(Information Ceiling).}\\;%\nIf constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), then $Y \\mathop{\\text{---}} T_{1:K} \\mathop{\\text{---}} C_{1:K}$ forms a Markov chain, and the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K};\\, Y) \\leq I(T_{1:K};\\, Y)$: self-extracted constraints cannot carry more information about $Y$ than the source traces. This bound holds for any model, task, and deterministic $f$, applying to any self-extraction pipeline. The contribution is the identification of the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain structure, establishing that constraints carry no information about $Y$ beyond what $A$ already provides.",
  "new_string": "\\textbf{Remark 1} \\textit{(Information Ceiling).}\\;%\nIf constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K};\\, Y) \\leq I(T_{1:K};\\, Y)$---a standard result for any deterministic post-processing. The contribution is identifying the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain structure: under the degeneration condition of Observation~1 ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), constraints are fully determined by answers, yielding the tighter bound $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ and the conditional independence $C \\perp Y \\mid A$ shown in Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}. This is an empirical conclusion holding under degeneration, not an unconditional structural claim."
}
```

_source line 14680 · 2026-05-23T10:42:40.767473+00:00_

34	\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}
35	\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\% standalone) amplifies the escape: combo gains reach $+$12.67\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+$11.67\,pp (LLaMA-8B), a $14\times$ amplification over zero-shot, with near-total error complementarity (Table~\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}, Appendix). Domain-adapted verification extends escape to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\%, consistent with the prediction that the capability gap can be shifted by increasing verifier accuracy. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}35\%$ NLI cost (Appendix~\ref{app:drc}).
36

_source line 14688 · 2026-05-23T10:43:01.009401+00:00_

9	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), effective sample size reduces to
10	\begin{equation}
11	\label{eq:effk}
12	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
13	\end{equation}
14	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}); e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.

50	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. We trace the failure mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.
51	
52	\looseness=-1 TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp---adopted as effects below one percentage point per class provide negligible practical benefit on datasets with $\geq$3 answer categories---confirm four conditions as equivalent; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}2$\,pp (MDE 1.86--4.84\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:power}). TOST sensitivity analysis across three margins ($\pm 1$, $\pm 2$, $\pm 3$\,pp) confirms that equivalence conclusions are margin-dependent (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise (mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). _source line 14698 · 2026-05-23T10:43:32.194042+00:00_

17	\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}
18	Scaling test-time compute via additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-verification and refinement \citep{weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}).
19

7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. The $\alpha$-insensitivity confirms degeneration: under $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ the penalty term vanishes and $\alpha$ becomes irrelevant, producing $\geq$99\% agreement across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ as a ceiling effect (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that BR reflects structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
10

5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.

_source line 14730 · 2026-05-23T10:45:42.774802+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). Observation~1 is specific to source-attribution scoring pipelines; the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies more broadly to any deterministic self-extraction. Empirical validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA paradigm.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This independence principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information. The principle applies to any self-verification method that draws on the generating model's own distribution, extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings: even provably sound formal verification inherits the model's distributional prior when constraints are self-extracted. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence, capability ordering, and task-verifier alignment (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open. The diagnostics we provide enable future work to recognize this limitation upfront.
10

完整标题: `Beyond Self-Consistency: Self-Extracted Constraints\\Provide No Detectable Improvement in LLM Reasoning`
理由: "Detectable" 精确绑定 power limitation（<21% for 2pp），比 "Show No Evidence" 更精炼。仍然去掉 "Logical"。

### 修正 2: Observation 1 格式（补充 Fix 2）
除了 Proposition→Observation 重命名外：
- **删除 ∎ (QED) 符号**——经验性观察不配 QED
- **"Proof." → "Empirical support."** 作为引导段标题
- 内容改为列出 >90% agreement 的经验支持数据，不再用 "By (i)... By (ii)..." 的证明格式

### 修正 3: Figure 3 C⊥Y|A 标注（新增，对应 W4）
**文件**: `analysis.tex` Figure 3 TikZ 代码（lines 62-108）
当前 Figure 3 DAG 中有标注 `$C \!\perp\! Y \mid A$`
**修改**:
(a) 标注改为 `$C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$`（加 approx 符号）
(b) caption 末尾加: "Approximate conditional independence ($C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$) is empirically supported by ${>}90\%$ argmax agreement (Observation~1) but is not a formal consequence of the DPI bound (Remark~1)."

**文件**: `analysis.tex` line 7
当前: "MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4% of problems"
需要加 scope 限定，因为 94.4% 是 Mistral-7B FOLIO 的 specific condition，而 abstract 说 "over 90%"（overall），会让读者困惑。

1	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. The $\alpha$-insensitivity confirms degeneration: under $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ the penalty term vanishes and $\alpha$ becomes irrelevant, producing $\geq$99\% agreement across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ as a ceiling effect (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that BR reflects structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unrelia

_source line 14774 · 2026-05-23T10:51:03.877479+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2

_source line 14796 · 2026-05-23T12:30:14.547101+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp.
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it

[Director 指令] [Patch — Round 18 升四路线] EMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL 5/25。Round 18 审稿 3.5→目标 4.0。以下 4 项内容修改，按优先级排序。引用不需要验证。

## Fix 1: SCB vs Huang et al. 新颖性论证（最高优先级）

**问题**: related_work.tex §2 "Distinction from CoT confirmation bias" 段落缺少与 Huang et al. (2024) 的 3 维度显式对比。当前 abstract 和 intro 用 "extending Huang's findings" 措辞，被 reviewer 认为是自我降级。

**修改要求**:
1. `related_work.tex` 第 13-14 行 "Distinction from CoT confirmation bias" 段落：补充一个 **3 维度显式对比**：
   - (a) 形式化：Huang 是 empirical observation；SCB 提供 Markov chain 形式化 (Observation 1, Remark 1)
   - (b) 诊断框架：Huang 没有可操作诊断工具；SCB 提供 3 个 zero-cost diagnostics (K_eff, BR, C_0)
   - (c) 排除范围：Huang 未测试 symbolic/formal verification；SCB 系统排除 27 种 training-free 变体
2. `abstract.tex` 第 1 行：把 "extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification" 改为更自信的表述，如 "providing independent formal evidence that extends beyond..."
3. `introduction.tex` 第 10 行：同样调整 "extends" 措辞为更自信表述
4. `discussion.tex` 第 9 行：同样调整

关键原则：**停止 "consistent with" / "extending" 自我降级**，改为明确声明 SCB 提供了 Huang 没有的东西。

## Fix 2: BR 重新框定为 degeneration 度量

**问题**: analysis.tex 中 BR 仍被称为 "bias ratio"，但 permutation null 已证明 BR_null ≈ BR_obs (p>0.7)，意味着 BR 完全由 base rate 解释，不是独立 bias 信号。

**修改要求**:
1. `analysis.tex` 第 9 行附近：permutation null 段落中添加 BR_excess = BR_obs - BR_null 的报告（数值：BR_excess ≈ 0，具体值从 "BR_null ≈ BR_obs" 推导）
2. 将 BR 框定调整：不改名（仍叫 bias ratio），但在描述中明确 BR 量化的是 "extraction degeneration degree"（约束退化到与 majority vote 等价的程度），而非独立的 bias 信号。当前第 9 行已有 "structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal" 表述，在此基础上强化为 degeneration framing。
3. 确保 `diagnosis.tex` 中 BR 相关描述与此一致

## Fix 3: Scope 边界明确化

**问题**: 论文在 SICA-specific 结论和 general 结论之间摇摆。

**修改要求**:
1. `discussion.tex`（当前仅 10 行）：扩展 scope 边界讨论，明确区分：
   - SICA-specific 结论：Observation 1 (source-attribution scoring 下的退化)、27 变体排除
   - General 结论：DPI bound (Remark 1) 适用于任何 deterministic self-extraction；SCB 跨 9 个模型普遍存在
   - Open questions：trained extractors 是否能打破 information ceiling；cross-architecture 方法的扩展性
2. 确保 abstract 和 conclusion 中的 claim scope 与 discussion 对齐

## Fix 4: Observation 1 补误差界 / 量化 degeneration

**问题**: Observation 1 ("Empirical support" 段落) 缺少量化精度。

**修改要求**:
1. `analysis.tex` 第 49-51 行 "Empirical support" 段落：补充具体数字说明 degeneration 程度（如 "Z3 rejects only 3.4% of constraints" → 量化 |C^∖| / |C| ≈ 0.034，confirming the degeneration condition |(C^∖)| ≈ 0"）
2. 补充 "over 90% of problems" 的具体数字（从 Tab:ablation 取：773/804 = 96.1% for Qwen, 类似数据）

所有修改后编译检查页数不超过 8 页正文。

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
5	\label{sec:pipeline}
6	\label{sec:condorcet}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), effective sample size reduces to
10	\begin{equation}
11	\label{eq:effk}
12	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
13	\end{equation}
14	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.
15	
16	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
19	
20	\looseness=-1
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds
…[truncated 3850 chars]

110	SICA effectiveness depends sharply on the accuracy regime (Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter}).
111	
112	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--60\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect.
113	
114	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%).} All nine Type~A conditions produce no significant positive effects. Even in-context learning with gold logical examples yields $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp (n.s.). Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$).
115	
116	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%).} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning. When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
117	
118	Four supporting analyses confirm the mechanism: constraint ablation shows both MAX-SAT and count-only each agree with SC on ${>}$96\% of predictions ($n = 804$; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}); 91.8\% of constraints are discriminative but mirror the majority; effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($+12.8$\,pp) with direction determined by accuracy regime; and hyperparameter peaks coincide with low SC baselines (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}).

50	
51	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Two conditions are jointly necessary for any positive trend: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
52	
53	This correlation manifests through a specific mechanism: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.
54	
55	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.85\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88{\times}^{\dag}$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ ($^{\dag}$based on 4/12 traces due to extraction failures; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that 75.9\% of the potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.
56

130	\label{sec:interventions}
131	
132	We test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.
133	
134	\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\%, but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models produce accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp of self-extraction. Multi-temperature sampling ($T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$) yields $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
135	
136	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\Delta = -9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\ref{app:remediation}). Ten further experiments (code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles) produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
137	
138	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, forming a same-model trade-off ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.85\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) suggests weaker models suffer more severe confirmation

```json
  "old_string": "Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. Methodologically, SCB is formalized via a Markov chain structure (Observation~1, Remark~1) and confirmed through systematic exclusion of 27 training-free alternatives (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The key distinction: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful; SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted.",
  "new_string": "Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \\citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. Three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). Empirical validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, extending \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open.",
  "new_string": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. We distinguish two levels of generality. \\emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape this collapse. \\emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline, and symbolic confirmation bias (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary, and how cross-architecture verification scales to stronger generators, remain open."
}
```

6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). 6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. We distinguish two levels of generality. \emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape this collapse. \emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline, and symbolic confirmation bias (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary, and how cross-architecture verification scales to stronger generators, remain open.
10
